- 2026-10-24 — [sheet] HP is required only when a sheet layout says so (required: true) — Rules-agnostic: not every game has hit points.
- 2026-10-24 — [items] Magic and cursed items start unidentified, showing appearsAs and what you notice; reveals are fact by fact; hidden bonuses don't touch the player's numbers (tentative) — Players infer from play; nothing is given away.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] Encumbrance is one optional-rules row and time lengths come from the rules; both leave campaign creation (M4b) — One place for rules, and fewer setup steps.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Send offers one choice, keep in Handouts or burn after reading; burnt messages never reach Handouts but stay in the GM's log; burning is text-only — No contradictory settings, and the GM keeps a record.
- 2026-10-24 — [data] The GM's direct uploads stay in app storage (GM only until revealed; images resized, PDFs ≤10 MB); Drive is for adventure packs — Mid-session photos shouldn't need Drive; storage stays small and protected.
- 2026-10-24 — [ui] Reveals show at once in quiet mode as one silent card; other alerts wait — The GM chose the moment; quiet still means silent.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] A location's encounter check replaces the GM's "Encounter check" reminders while the party is there; "Use my check here instead"; no backlog on leaving; Explore shows the source — One active check, and the GM stays in control.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] The Reviewer may read the ID plan (ids, kinds, pages) and also counts from the PDF itself — It can catch omissions in both the pack and the plan without seeing the builders' reasoning.
- 2026-10-24 — [expedition] The expedition clock is one seconds counter; rounds, turns, hours and days are worked out from it (04 updated in M5 step 2; technical, decided in Claude Code with Francis) — One number makes jumps, undo and odd turn lengths exact.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Roster entries carry npcId or an inline name (never both); "Make NPC" promotes one — Most listed people are a single line; promote them when they matter.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Rumour truth is always a string: "true", "false" or "partial"; plain booleans are converted at import — One type to check, without failing hand-written packs.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] option links any adventure entry to its switch ("id" or "id=choice"); dependents follow their parent; same meaning on tables everywhere — Switches must be explicit and consistent.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Adventure mechanics have no ruleKey (one switch each) and a display-only category — ruleKey merges choices, which would turn several mechanics off at once.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] Treasure uses value in coins (whole numbers) instead of valueGp; valueGp is converted at import — Copper and silver amounts can't be whole numbers of gold.
- 2026-10-24 — [ip] The private test setting pack's notes copied from the book are rewritten in your own words before table test 1, not now (Francis) — the M4 gate comes first; the pack is private, so nothing leaked.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Adventure packs aim for completeness: mechanics in full, printed page refs everywhere, own-words descriptions with clue fields, exact quotes only where wording is the clue, images from the owner's copy (GM-only crops of read-aloud and room text) — Clues must survive conversion and the GM can run from the app without copying prose.
- 2026-10-24 — [process] New milestone M8b (Adventures) before table test 1, which now runs a published adventure; handouts and maps move from M9 — Running a real adventure is the best test; a dedicated milestone keeps M5–M8 stable.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Pack images live in the GM's Google Drive (GM device only); reveals copy into app storage under access rules; step-by-step guide on first use — Free space and the owner's files stay theirs, while hidden stays hidden.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Adventure content is GM-only by default; locations have a player layer shown when opened; Handouts keeps revealed images, kept text and introduced people — A short player reference without leaks; the GM's voice stays the main channel.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] The converter becomes a manager with five specialists (Reader, Mechanics, Places, Images, Reviewer); the Reviewer is a fresh agent; PyMuPDF; at most two review rounds — Focused parts, links kept by an ID plan, and the pack is never graded by its builder.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] Format version 3 for adventure kinds; meta.id (all packs), meta.code prefix, meta.requires, meta.pageOffset — Stable references, and an adventure can't silently override setting entries.
- 2026-10-24 — [expedition] Hour time unit (exact minutes underneath); +1 hour in the wilderness (M5); Travel with editable time (M8b) — Wilderness travel is measured in hours.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Encounter checks sit on locations (onlyAt day/night, byTrack); Day / Night switch, Check now, active check shown — Adventures time their checks by place, time of day and countdowns.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Shops: the shop's price wins, stock runs out, text prices, services logged; rumours: told ones marked, truth GM-only — Shopping and rumours as the book runs them, without leaks.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Tracks have rosters with pack-defined states, several-at-once ticks, next-free-entry and a per-change nudge; the timeline is day-based, with Start adventure setting day 1 — The countdown and roster run in a few taps, dice at the table.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Images need alt text; monsters, people, items and places can link an image; only useful art is packed — Show players what they see, accessibly, without bloat.
- 2026-10-24 — [sheet] Pre-mades are offered at every character creation if unclaimed and not dead, and always need GM approval — Fast replacements in deadly play, with the GM in control.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] References between packs use meta.id and resolve through the priority order; adventures carry their own printed stat blocks — Homebrew edits apply and renames don't break links.
- 2026-10-24 — [adventure] Every automated adventure mechanic can be turned off (adventure-mechanics optional rules; During play switches); defaults on — GMs who prefer paper aren't forced into it.
- 2026-10-24 — [packs] Coverage report: summary first, full prep checklist, 8-word copying check, 5 spot checks minimum, delivered privately; prep mode in M8b — Prep is when the GM wants depth, and fixes land in one place.

- 2026-10-22 — [testing] M4b gate set: pack fighter and magic-user in under 3 minutes each, kit, GM approval, equip warnings, fill, all 22 classes by automated test — proves guided creation is fast and the pack is complete

- 2026-10-22 — [perm] M3 fresh-session review: nothing critical; fixed before the m3 tag, each pinned by `supabase/tests/m3-review.test.ts`: (1) a library entry can't move to another pack or kind; (2) a campaign's homebrew goes only with its campaign (no deleting it alone, which erased its entries unlogged) and a retired campaign's homebrew can't change; (3) a retired campaign's files are frozen too; (4) the GM's exceptions for GM-only entries moved to a GM-only table (campaign_pack_secrets, like actor_secrets) and earlier log lines about them became the GM's — players had been able to read those entries' names, against "hidden means hidden"; (5) the Rules library and pickers show only your own packs, not your GM's; (6) a campaign's homebrew can be attached only to that campaign; (7) only a pack leaving a campaign can be logged as "library.delete"; plus formulas with absurd answers stay text (never "Infinity"), the test stand-in database follows the same rules, and an invented name replaced a famous one in a test
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 converter skill test: the skill is updated to the real format (tabletop-pack version 2, every kind, formulas, values per class, option notes) and checks its output with `npm run pack:check`, which runs the app's own reader and editor checks in Node (no new dependency). Tested on one real section (one encounter sub-table section: 4 tables, 80 rows, 72 linked to the test pack's monsters; private, in packs/). Running the checker on the full private test pack found the editor stricter than real packs; fixed: null counts as empty, text where a number could be stays text, values per class are kept, ability rows use `note` with level and description optional, XP tables need no name, lookup rows may hold several values — the editor must open whatever a valid pack holds
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 7c, editor for the last kinds: tables (plain: ranges, result, how many, monster; lookup: key and value; matrix rows kept as they are), treasure types, rules sets (time, coins, starting money; the rest kept), optional rules (choices with ids made from names, a default among them, whether it can change later), hooks as text, sheet layouts. Kinds without names of their own (treasure types, hooks, sheet layouts) need none. Optional rules never compete, so the browser offers editing only the campaign's own homebrew ones. On a phone, row boxes name themselves (headings above wrapped boxes would mislead) — the editor now covers every kind, as decided 2026-10-21
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 7b, editor for classes, races, spells, spell lists and starting kits: linked entries are chosen from a list (one) or typed by name (several), stored as ids, with unknown names caught before saving; durations are a number or formula plus a unit; a class's progression, armour and weapon rules and other deep parts aren't in the form yet and are kept untouched, with the form naming them — no half-edited structures, nothing lost; no database change
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 7a, the in-app editor: one form engine driven by each kind's field list (not a hand-made form per kind), writing the pack format itself and keeping keys it doesn't show; entries are checked before saving. Everything is saved in the campaign's own homebrew; editing another pack's entry makes a homebrew version with the same id that wins, so the original stays untouched and a newer file never wipes an edit (Francis). Homebrew entries are now part of the campaign's record: logged (players see lines for player-facing kinds only), undoable, and locked when the campaign is retired — the database does it. New entries get an id from their name, never one another pack already uses. Split into 7a (items, stat blocks, conditions, light types, XP tables), 7b and 7c — "everything logged" and reviewable steps
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 6, the formula evaluator: a small parser of our own (no dependency, pack text never run as code) for numbers, level, + - * / × ÷, brackets, floor() and dice; it gives a whole number or a dice expression and never rolls. Fractions round down, marked "(rounded down)" (Francis) — whole numbers for game values, the usual old-school convention, and never a silent surprise. Unreadable formulas stay as written. Capped at levels 1–99, 100 dice, 200 characters. Shown in the setting-rules browser with an "At level" picker on entries that have formulas (a player starts at their character's level); level-up and character creation use the same evaluator later — one evaluator, tested once
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 5, stacking: one order per campaign, top wins an entry two packs share (same kind and id); ↑ ↓ on each pack in Campaign → Setting rules (logged, undoable as one step). Homebrew on top by default; newly added setting rules go on top but under the homebrew at the top. Per-entry exceptions live in the setting-rules browser, not Optional rules (Francis): read the other versions, "Use this version", "Back to the stack order"; stored on the winning pack's link with the entry's name for the log. Optional rules never compete. The campaign's own homebrew pack is made on first save (no empty pack in every campaign), is named "<campaign> homebrew", sits on top, is hidden from the Rules library and can't be removed; the database allows one per campaign and only its GM makes it — clashes are resolved where they're seen, one rule decides everywhere
- 2026-10-22 — [words] M3 step 3g, from testing: the account ⋯ menu's "Your setting rules" is renamed **Rules library** (it's the account's packs, reused across campaigns; Campaign → Setting rules is what one campaign uses) — the two names read as the same thing. Optional rules lose their always-visible "Can change later / Changing later warns" text and the standing mid-campaign warning: every rule's name gets an ⓘ that shows whether it can change later plus its notes; only "Locked" stays visible (greyed buttons need a reason). Changing a "warn" rule in a running campaign asks first (Change it / Keep), once per rule while the optional rules are open, including through "Use all defaults" — the warning shows when it matters instead of cluttering the screen
- 2026-10-22 — [ui] M3 step 4, the setting-rules browser: read only, in three places (Campaign → Setting rules → Browse these rules; Browse on each pack in Your setting rules; a player's ⋯ → Setting rules, also on Your characters). Folded groups per kind with counts, a name search, details in plain words built from whatever an entry holds (no per-system screens), formulas shown as text until the evaluator (step 6). Optional rules aren't listed there. No database change: who sees which kinds was already enforced. The R1 design has no browser screen, so this follows the Setting rules section's look — canvas to be updated — packs differ too much between systems for hand-made layouts per kind, and a calm list beats a long scroll
- 2026-10-22 — [core] M3 step 3f, retiring campaigns (Francis's request): the GM retires a campaign and can bring it back. Retired means read only for everyone (players keep reading and downloading) and nobody can join; it folds into a collapsed "Retired" section on the picker. The database refuses every change in a retired campaign with a plain message — except the GM bringing it back (and nothing else in the same change), and deletes that follow from elsewhere (setting rules deleted from a library, the campaign itself). Logged as campaign.retire / campaign.restore. The app refuses too, so the main buttons (Invite a player, Make your character, adding setting rules, renaming) hide; anything not yet hidden gets the plain message — campaigns end without losing their history, and clutter leaves the list
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 3e, fixes from testing: adding setting rules to a running campaign (from the library or a file) opens their optional rules first, every one open, so locked rules can be chosen once; cancelling after an import keeps the pack in the library only. A rule with notes (and choices with notes) has its name as a quiet button that shows them underneath; nothing extra shows otherwise. "Your setting rules" is in the ⋯ menu only for someone who is GM of a campaign — players never need it, and starting a campaign still offers importing
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 3d, deleting setting rules: "Delete…" on each pack in Your setting rules. Unused: a plain confirm. Used: the warning names each campaign and lists what it loses, built from what's in the pack; typing the pack's name (capitals don't matter) unlocks "Delete for good". Each campaign's link keeps the pack's name (set by the database from the pack, never changed), so the log reads "Priya deleted the setting rules River Rules from their library…" even after the pack is gone; that removal can't be undone. Adding, changing optional rules of, and removing setting rules now have their own log lines too (until now they read "changed something") — a log line nobody can read isn't a record
- 2026-10-22 — [ui] M3 step 3c, starting a campaign: nothing is saved until the end (Back keeps what was entered; "Create campaign now" on any step keeps the defaults for the steps left). Setting rules picked later sit on top. Optional rules are all open while starting, locked ones included. Encumbrance and time lengths come from the picked setting rules' rules sets after the optional rules chosen, otherwise the app's defaults: no encumbrance; 6 seconds, 10 minutes, 24 hours. A day must be a whole number of turns, like a turn of rounds. If the campaign is made but something after fails, the GM is told what to add from Campaign → Setting rules — a half-made campaign is impossible to miss, and nothing is guessed
- 2026-10-22 — [ui] Setting rules screen differences from the R1 design for M3 step 3b: the section heading "Setting rules" covers the attached packs and Character fields below them; each pack shows its version and counts, "Optional rules · N changed", "Update from a new file" (packs from your own library) and "Remove…" (a confirm says it stays in your library); "Add more setting rules" offers "From your library" (an inline list with Add) and "Import a file" (imports into your library and attaches in one go); optional rules use the design's choice buttons with the default marked, locked ones disabled and labelled "Locked", and a warning line once a "warn" rule is changed — canvas to be updated
- 2026-10-22 — [perm] Packs and library entries each have one reading rule instead of two (the security advisor's performance note); who can see what is unchanged
- 2026-10-22 — [perm] M3 step 3a: setting rules attach to campaigns through `campaign_packs` (optional-rule choices start at their defaults; the newest attached sits on top). The GM attaches only packs from their own library; members read the pack's name and its player-facing entries (classes, races, items, spells, spell lists, conditions, light types, starting kits, optional rules); the GM reads all of it. Optional rules with `changeLater` "never" are treated as locked, like "lock". Attaching, changing optional rules and detaching are logged; an optional-rule change can be undone — the campaign's rules are visible to its players without exposing the GM's secrets
- 2026-10-22 — [process] The hook runner (fill, warn, factor, remind) moves from M3 to M4b, with the decision on hook `test` syntax; M3 builds the formula evaluator and shows each pack's hooks as readable text — a runner can't be built for a syntax still left open (supersedes the 2026-10-21 ruling to build the hook kinds in M3)
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] (Same ruling as M3 step 3d above, as written in the design Project.) The GM can delete setting rules from their library. Attached to no campaign: a plain confirm. Attached: a warning lists each campaign affected and what it loses (its optional rules, the rules behind characters' classes and races, which keep their names, and its items, monsters and tables), and the GM types the pack's name to confirm; each affected campaign's log records the removal — clutter goes in one place, never by an accidental tap, and campaign history stays complete
- 2026-10-22 — [packs] M3 step 2: Markdown packs are read by the same importer as JSON (a file named .md, or starting with "#", is Markdown). Markdown entry ids come from their names ("Rope, 50 ft" → rope-50-ft); `## field:` entries become one sheet layout named after the pack; weights must be in coins and time in rounds, turns, minutes, hours or days, otherwise the value is listed and left out — deterministic, and nothing is guessed or dropped silently
- 2026-10-22 — [data] M3 step 1: the library is account-level and private (tables `packs` and `library_entries`, owner only until packs are attached to campaigns); importing or updating saves the pack and all its entries in one database step (`import_pack`, running as the caller); updating from a newer file replaces changed entries, adds new ones and removes ones no longer in the file, after a preview listing all three; library changes aren't campaign events, so they aren't in a campaign's log — what's in your library is yours alone, and a pack is never half-saved

- 2026-11-01 — [packs] One name for "only while this optional rule is on": `option` on every entry, tables included; `onlyWithOption` is read as an alias and the checker stops asking for it (07 §14 and Exploration; G-40 ruled; a builder step) — two names for one idea kept tripping the converter.

- 2026-11-01 — [packs] Faction stances are data on the faction, `factions[].relations { factionId: stance }`, not a matrix table (07 §5; G-33 ruled, shown in the GM's faction panel in M8b) — R2's reputation will want it on the faction.
- 2026-11-01 — [packs] Shops are for things a character buys: a town's tax offices and its guard aren't shops; their figures stay as GM notes on the buildings (byzantine-dungeon, three planned shops dropped) — a Buy button on a poll tax makes no sense.
- 2026-11-01 — [perm] `groups_meeting` stays as it is: players may learn that two groups are in the same place (Join up showing or disappearing says as much), even while both places are hidden; which place stays hidden (05 "Split the party"; the M5 review's ⚑) — "no need to hide that info".
- 2026-11-01 — [perm] Message attachments (B3, M5b g4): only the GM may attach a file to a message, a picture up to 10 MB; players send text only; the database and the bucket enforce both (05 Messaging; 03) — a player's picture would need the GM's eye first, and 10 MB takes a phone photo without shrinking it.
- 2026-11-01 — [process] Vercel previews are built on request: a pull request gets one only while it carries the **preview** label (`.github/workflows/preview.yml`); building one on every push used up Vercel's free 100 deployments a day, which production shares (docs/testing.md, Deploying) — "Yes" to building only when asked.
- 2026-11-01 — [process] Merging no longer requires a branch to be up to date with main (the "Protect main" ruleset's setting is off); a push to main runs check, database and layout on what the merge brought in, and production deploys only if they pass. The **database** job skips changes made only of screen files, layout tests, pack scripts and CI extras (docs/testing.md, The GitHub check) — to save GitHub minutes (2,400 on 2026-11-01, mostly re-runs after updates) without weakening the checks.
- 2026-11-02 — [process] The CI review (`.github/workflows/review.yml`) runs once per pull request (opened, ready, reopened), not on every push, and again when the **review** label is added; Sonnet by default, Opus when `supabase/` changes. Its spend is capped at [amount removed] a month in the Anthropic console (Francis). Why: at the day's pace a run per push could cost far more than the review is worth as a backup to the required checks.

- 2026-11-04 — [items] A magic item whose ordinary form the pack lacks names the nearest listed item as `baseId` and says why in its note (mb-2) — The app needs a base for class lists and damage; standing checker warnings teach people to ignore them — `design/rulings/m5b-magic-items-base-2026-11-04.md`

- 2026-11-09 — [process] Gallery pictures: merges no longer wait for Design, except for a picture change the pull request didn't announce. The first pictures of a new screen, component or variant that an approved mockup or a ruling describes merge like expected changes; with neither they stop as a scope check, like an unruled layout or wording change or an open ⚑. The builder adds the design-look label at the merge and Design removes it after its look; nothing rejected or not yet looked at is open when a gate run, a gate sitting or a player test starts or a milestone is tagged, and merges that change pictures wait during a sitting or a player test. The 2026-11-05 limits return when pre-test hardening begins, unless Francis rules otherwise (Francis, limits by Design) — Since 5 November picture pull requests took a median 1.6 h from the ready label to the merge against 0.6 h without, 7 of the 24 took 5 to 17 h, and there is no regular play on the live site before table test 1; Design's look still catches real slips, so it stays, after the merge — `docs/testing.md` (The component gallery)

- 2026-11-11 — [ui] The marching order is drawn as party circles in ranks: the portrait or initials in the centre (two letters where initials clash), a still halo with its own token and a ring that empties for a light, statuses under the name (on a phone a rank of three drops the word), a held circle washed and lifted, hit points beside the formation as pips (mc-1 … mc-6) — "The very first concept we had for this app" (Francis, on mockup `scene/01`) — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [ui] The Scene's procedures run as step sequences in a panel at the right edge (the whole screen on a phone): an answer moves on with Undo, the rule one tap away formatted like the books (rt-1); camp ends at the watches and the night is the GM's, opened by "Go to sleep" and left by "Dawn, Day Four"; players see their own steps and a Meanwhile line; the day's travel is one simple drawing of the way, marked; the top bar hides on scroll (mc-7 … mc-14) — Camp as built was "long and very difficult to digest or understand step by step" (Francis, sitting 1) — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [ui] The app is built primarily for the iPad and the PC, the phone a distant third (mc-9) — Every player so far used a computer away from the table; Francis, on the Scene mockups — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [perm] A travel group's log lines are read by the players who were in the group when each was written, and the GM (fm-3) — "Whoever is in the group now" would hand a group's past to late joiners and to a later group given the same number — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] In fm-1: travel lines from before it stay the GM's; only the five travel commands' lines are the group's (an activity's cost keeps the GM's line); a marcher who has left the group can't undo the march (fm-4 … fm-6) — Built where the rulings were silent; confirmed by Francis — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [perm] Players can't read a pack patch aimed at an item no pack contains; the older database test is updated to match (hd-1) — The hidden-data fix after the look-back reviews (F58, F83) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [testing] M5b is tagged only once both hidden-data fixes are live (the pack cache and patches; the place being entered, fm-2); the walk can pass before them (gt-1) — "Hidden data shown" blocks the tag in the gate plan, and one of the two was live — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [perm] A reveal of a hidden pack entry belongs to the campaign it was made in (hd-2) — A reveal in one campaign opened the entry in every campaign on the same pack (look-back review of #554) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] Encounter check (Check now) respects the hour: at night only the night's checks and those with no time limit, by day only the day's (nc-1; changes DG3 of 2026-10-30) — At the night stop the day's check was answered in the night's place, so "Which watch?" never showed (rewalk, sitting 1) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] A night check that hits at a place with its own tables suggests that place's night table first, else the terrain's (nc-2) — The pack wrote the place's night table for that moment; at the walk a "(day)" table opened — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] Night, for encounter checks, runs from the setting's sunset to its sunrise, else 22:00 to 06:00 by the clock; the night stop always counts as night; no switch (nc-3) — Nothing told the Scene it was night, so every check counted as a day check (the Builder, sizing nc-1) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [testing] For fm-1, the hidden-data fix and ac-1, the first database proof is CI's local database; TEST gets one batched turn after CI is green and before main (db-2); an existing test changed by ruling on a fix branch moves to a non-fix branch cut from it (tg-1) — TEST was blocked by two unmerged pull requests' migrations, and each pull request gets a CI run anyway — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [testing] The TEST push script accepts migrations TEST already holds from other open pull requests (db-1); each TEST turn is still checked with Francis first — TEST held two unmerged pull requests' migrations and refused every other branch's (F82) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] The place a group is entering is the GM's until shown to that group, in the database: the GM's name and the place's id are kept in a GM-only row and players read "the next place" (fm-2, in fm-1's migration) — The GM's name for an unshown place was stored where the group's players read it until morning (look-back review of #528) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [perm] In an activity one character does, a player names only their own characters as the one who acts and finds (the GM names anyone); an activity several or the whole party do stays open to the travel group; enforced in the database (ac-1) — A player could act for another player's character and put the yield in their pack (found by the Builder after sitting 1) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [process] The path to first play on 5 December: build in the order the players meet things and run by hand whatever is not reached: creation (the cleric's patron step, no mixing, level 2) by 1 December; places picked from the pack with their content; the port as a settlement with buying; point-to-point travel in turns; encounters; finds and the Pack tab; an XP tally; fights as the reach goal; the Scene pieces on that path are built straight into the redesigned form — Francis, planning session — `plans/december-first-play.md`, `design/notes/planning-2026-11-11-answers.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [ui] The Scene pages on the December path: the town page as its calmer variant (safety and encounters behind one fact, a place's row with its eye only), a place, person or table opening in the right panel, a place's aspects as pieces, no People section, the accent also on table bars, links and place numbers; one Move on with its checks named and problem ways listed; one Start session; the encounter check as a pop-up that opens by itself (mc-15 … mc-27) — Francis, "yes to all" on the pictures of `scene/png/tour/`; built straight into the redesigned form for 5 December — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [items] The Pack rework's first rulings: the GM hands out a find from the place's treasure rows on the Scene, by a sheet that sends it to a character (then which) or to the party stash (pk-1); a find or a thing taken from the stash lands loose and the player puts it away (pk-2); every stash row ends in Take, with Undo (pk-3); the party stash is a screen of its own showing who put each thing in and when (pk-4); a find is announced to the player by a card and a count on the Pack's icon (pk-5) — Francis, on the Pack mockups (`mockups/pack/`), for the first night of the December campaign — `design/rulings/pack-rework-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [process] Table test 1 is replaced by live play sessions: the December campaign's sessions (the first on 5 December, the second two to three weeks or more after, re-planned after each) are R1's play tests; 08 is re-forecast on that footing — "Table test is gone, replaced by live play sessions" (Francis, planning session) — `design/notes/planning-2026-11-11-answers.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [core] A person's name is never the front of their email: the name box starts empty, an account with no chosen name shows as "A player", and a name can be changed later from the menu (nm-1) — A player showed to the table as the front of an email address (rewalk, sitting 1, F73); players asked to change their name later — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [adventure] A rumour is given by tapping its row: a sheet asks whether it goes to a character or the party, its words are recorded in that character's or the party's notes, and whether it is true stays the GM's (tb-1) — Each player rolls a rumour at the first night's meet-up; a table opened from the list was read-only (Francis, planning session) — `design/rulings/pack-rework-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [testing] M5b is not tagged until F86 (no Rest card at camp after a forced march that paid off a move) and F87 (the clock and the travel day out of step after an Undo and a new Next day) are understood and fixed (gt-2) — Each loses track of something the rules depend on (sitting 2) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [process] The Builder may read two records of the live database, read-only, for the gate-test campaign, to trace F86 and F87 (db-3) — Francis's OK; production reads are refused by default — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] A wilderness expedition can go into a dungeon without ending: Move on offers going inside, switching to turns, and coming out switches back (mc-29; with the redesigned Move on, after the December campaign's first night) — The party walked to a ruin and could not go in by the turn (sitting 2, F90) — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [ui] The day drives, for the GM: when a step of the day's procedure is finished the next opens by itself, each sheet with Not now; players get one plainly named prompt that waits and nothing opens by itself for them; the Scene is where things rest between moves (mc-30) — "It needs to step the players/GM through the process sheet by sheet, only defaulting back to Scene between moves" (Francis, sitting 2) — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [perm] Watches stay shared: any player of the group arranges anyone's watch, an exception to ac-1 (ws-1) — At a table one person writes down what the group agreed (Francis, after sitting 2) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [packs] The pack format gains, all optional: a table row's creatures with their own counts (pf-2); a place's aspects as pieces with a bold lead-in (pf-3); a town's price mark-up (pf-4); and a town laid out as the source lays one out: an identity line, facts, headed prose, the watch, lodgings with a grade, a person's portrait and fixed-order leads, and `[[kind:id|label]]` links (pf-5) — "Follow the formatting from the [fae-woodland] source material and ensure that those elements are defined" (Francis, for the December port) — `design/rulings/dec-pack-formats-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [adventure] A route's `travel` may list ways of moving, each with its own time (`by`, `name`, `value`, `unit`); a move between linked places sets the place and advances the clock in one step with one Undo, running each turn's procedure as Advance by N does, then puts the encounter check on the card; hidden links stay the GM's (pt-f1 … pt-f3, the first cut of point-to-point travel) — The December campaign's first night is an adventure of linked spots moved between in turns — `design/rulings/dec-point-travel-format-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [expedition] In a mode whose step is a day or longer, the clock's hour doesn't decide day or night for encounter checks: it is day until the night stop (nc-3a, the lead's reading of nc-3) — A campaign's clock starts at 00:00 and a day mode moves in whole days, so by the hour alone every check would be a night check (the Builder, building nc-1) — `design/rulings/m5b-morning-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-11 — [perm] Players see the ways on from where the party is (where each leads, or "not yet explored"), never a link the GM keeps hidden, and may tap a way to propose it to the GM, who makes the move (mc-28; changes "GM only" of 2026-10-30) — "a, one tap away propose" (Francis) — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [packs] The pack format gains hoards, verbatim hooks, map keys on the picture, an availability chance, `sameAs` for one item in two packs with the campaign's pack order deciding, shareable sections, and regions (a place holding places, with its map, rules, tables, default ways of travelling, a check on each move, and barriers on links); and the converter has a ten-line standard for maps that draw no links (pf-6 … pf-12) — "Is regions a good way to organize this kind of information?" and "will this be always difficult for pack creator?" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-pack-formats-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [adventure] Regions are the organising model for places: any depth of nesting, a region's rules beside the campaign's, and never the word "Region" on screen (rg-1 … rg-3) — Francis, on the regions proposal — `design/rulings/dec-pack-formats-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [process] The December plan's ranking is accepted as proposed; shop stock is tracked for the day only and resets once the party has left; a map is shown whole, its key being part of the picture; both go to design sessions before building — Francis, on the sized plan sheet — `design/notes/planning-2026-11-11-answers.md` (37 to 39)

- 2026-11-12 — [expedition] Next day starts each group's travel day with the clock and one Undo takes both back; after a move on the new day that Undo is refused whole, naming what stands in the way where cheap (ud-1, ud-2; changes #617's Undo for that case) — No Rest card after a forced march and a clock out of step with the travel day (sitting 2, F86 and F87): the travel day was only started by the GM's travel card when it was on screen — `design/rulings/m5b-travel-day-undo-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [testing] For the travel day's fix, the two new failing tests are reworded in their own never-pushed commit to tap the real Next day with the GM's screen elsewhere; #617's own test, changed by ud-1, is changed on a non-fix branch cut from the fix branch (ud-3, under bq-6 and tg-1) — Technical, Francis's say on the Builder's flag — `design/rulings/m5b-travel-day-undo-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [adventure] An adventure's or a region's own rule takes the place of the setting's rule on the same matter where the pack says so, and the screen says which is in force; first use: a region's encounter check on each move stands in for the mode's scheduled check (rg-4, pf-12b; changes rg-2's "never replace") — "Adventure and region specific rules trump setting rules in most cases" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-pack-formats-2026-11-11.md`

- 2026-11-12 — [ui] Players see how each way on is travelled and how long it takes, for every way they are shown; a hidden way and a barrier's note stay the GM's (mc-28a) — At a table the GM says this before the party chooses, and choosing between a quick way and a slow one is the point (Francis, "B") — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] No way on is named to a group until something of the place they are standing in has been shown to them (mc-28b) — A known far end would tell players where they are before the GM has described it (Francis, "a") — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] A hidden way stays the GM's until revealed and is then listed for the players like any other; the action that reveals one is built after the December campaign's first night (mc-28c) — "If GM reveals the knowledge, they'd know it" (Francis) — `design/rulings/scene-mockup-rulings-2026-11-11.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] A place that holds places has its map as the body of its page on an iPad and a PC; the map is the pack's own picture untouched, opens whole on a tap with zoom, drag and Fit, and for players sits in a frame opened on where the party is (mc-31, mc-32, mc-37, mc-43, mc-44) — Francis, on the December mockups: "the map should be the exact image from the book" — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] A place's knowledge and rules are the book's full words set out as the book sets them, never trimmed by default; knowledge folds under its headings with the book's first line; the GM may share any section (mc-33, mc-34, mc-35, mc-40; changes pf-11) — "Full verbatim text and source formatting to the greatest extent possible" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-2026-11-12.md`

- 2026-11-12 — [ui] A destination is one row with its ways as modes; a proposed way reaches the GM as one sheet that shows any obstacle before the answer, asked "Do they get through?" - 2026-11-12 — [ui] A toggle carries no state words beside it; the eye is an open eye when shown and a closed eye when hidden, with no box and no word (ds-1a, ds-2) — "Player knows what a toggle does!"; "just the eye" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] Standards from the December mockups: no line explains what the screen already shows; a toggle glows softly when on; the matching die glyph goes wherever a die is named or a roll is called for; a sheet with an X has no Close button; a table's headings sit over a stronger rule (mc-46, ds-1b, ds-3, mc-43a, mc-48) — Francis, on set 1's last look — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] On a place of linked spots: spots are numbered as the map's key; a way's mode is a stacked button at the right of its row under "Where to?"; a proposed way opens its sheet by itself, counted and worked first in, first out; a rule's action button is an icon with no words; the players' "Activities" are an aid that opens each rule; players see the encounter check as the GM does (mc-47, mc-36a, mc-42a, mc-49, mc-45a, mc-39b, mc-50) — Francis, on set 1's last look — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [sheet] A patron is chosen only under the campaign's "Expanded cleric creation" option, in a creation step of its own straight after Class, and shows on the review as a line with its own Change; the paladin and the drow choose one as the cleric and the acolyte do; switching the option off hides a patron and keeps it (pt-9 … pt-14; changes the patron's place in `m5b-patrons-2026-11-07.md`) — "Select patron should be its own step, after selecting 'cleric' as a class" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-patrons-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-10 — [process] Each code pull request gets one local review before it merges: the coordinating Builder session runs the `reviewer` agent (Opus, medium) against `REVIEW.md`; docs-only and scripts-only pull requests are skipped; migrations and access rules are always reviewed; the findings are one comment on the pull request and a line in its status file, and for a pull request that draws a screen the comment names the mockup or ruling it was compared against (Design's ask, accepted). The CI review stays while its credit is topped up; whether to keep both is a cost question for Project Review (Francis, proposed by the Builder) — No pull request merged from 6 to 10 November had a review of either kind, the CI review failing quietly on an empty API credit; the first local review found two bugs in #596 before it merged — `.claude/README.md`, `REVIEW.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [process] Mockups first is written into CLAUDE.md, REVIEW.md and the builder's instructions: every plan piece names its approved mockup and the date, or says "none: data and logic only"; a visible change with no approved mockup is an Important review finding; a plan is merged to main before a builder branches from it; a pull request that conflicts with main gets main merged in before its final push; and the local review rule of 10 November is merged — Nine pull requests and 7,822 added lines were built without an approved mockup, and five pushes started no CI run because their pull requests conflicted (Project Review's report of 2026-11-12; Francis, "yes") — `health/project-review-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] "Never colour alone" is relaxed for everything: colour may carry a distinction by itself where its mockup shows it with the hue taken away, the contrast is measured in both modes and Francis approves it; a stat block's labels are coloured at the regular weight, with a label colour of its own in light mode (cr-c1, cr-c2; changes hard rule 7) — "Include my ruling to relax the rules - for everything not just stat blocks. Note I myself am partially colorblind and a good judge of this" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-colour-rule-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] The steps of play are a reminder, not a process: a strip of icons on the GM's and the players' Scene showing the procedure in force (the place's, else its mode's, else the app's default five), with no heading, no state and nothing that stops play; a step is a tap only where a pack carries its text; on a phone the strip is icons only (mc-53 … mc-56, mc-60; the guided turn of set 2's first drawing is dropped) — "I love the strip… as a reminder to the gm and to players of the flow of a turn, not because i want it systemetized" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-set2-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [expedition] The Scene has one time control, "Next turn" with an hourglass: one tap, one unit of the mode in force, with Undo; the menu's "Advance by…" stays; the GM's Scene carries the place's map opened on the party (mc-57, mc-58) — "One turn at a time is fine that's how it's done at the table" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-set2-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [adventure] A place's rules are open to players by default once the party is there; the pack marks a rule that is the GM's alone, and the GM may open or close any; a rule's button opens the rule and runs nothing, for the GM and the players (mc-51, mc-49a, pf-12d) — "This is not a hidden rule - players should be aware of it before they fall in" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-set2-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] One rule button sits beside Move on; two or more sit in a row under it, right-justified (mc-52) — Three beside Move on squeezed the place's name; "right justify the buttons when they're under move on" (Francis) — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-set2-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [ui] The app's icons are medieval or fantasy in nature and come from one open-licence set (game-icons.net, CC BY 3.0), each credited to its author; the menu gains "About this app" above Sign out, with the credits as a section inside it; icons already in the app change at the sweep (ds-5, ds-6, ds-6a) — Hand-drawn line icons read poorly; one source keeps one style and one licence (Francis: "b", "yes", "Put it in a subsection of new menu item 'About this app'") — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-set2-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [packs] `procedure` keeps its list shape and is allowed on a place and on the rules set, with an optional `text` for a step; a place's rule may be marked `gmOnly`; every icon name a pack already uses keeps resolving, mapped inside the app (pf-14, pf-12d, pf-12c) — The key was already live in two packs in that shape (the Pack Creator's correction); packs are not renamed for the new icons — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-set2-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [process] Until 5 December the coordinating Builder runs database sessions itself for add-only migrations and reports after; Francis's yes still comes first for anything that drops, renames or deletes data or changes who may read what; a session stops on a failed backup or anything unannounced in a dry run or the advisors; a backup before every main push stays; the main push is Francis's again on the 5th (db-s1 … db-s6) — "App is not 'live' until dec 5. we can loosen those restrictions a bit" (Francis) — `design/rulings/process-db-sessions-until-dec-5-2026-11-12.md`
- 2026-11-12 — [adventure] "Calls to adventure" opens from the card of the place the party is in and lists the campaign's adventures, each with its own hooks and rumours (a single adventure opens directly); a hook's row names what it pays and who gives it and where; a hook, a rumour or a posted notice is given to a character or the party and reaches a player as a card already kept in Notes (mc-61 … mc-67) — Francis, on the December mockups' set 3 — `design/rulings/dec-mockup-rulings-sets3-5-2026-11-12.md`

- 2026-11-21 — [testing] The private-names scan on GitHub: every pull request is scanned in the check's **changes** job for what it adds (added lines, new files' names, commit messages, title, text, branch name) against the repository secret `PRIVATE_NAMES` (the private lists, one name per line); a hit says the file and line, never the name, and fails check, database and layout; our own pull requests fail without the list, a fork's or Dependabot's passes unscanned with a note. Until it is on `main`, no cloud builder writes code. Cloud briefs say `npm ci`, never `npm install`, which rewrote `package-lock.json` in a cloud container. (Francis's message to the driver, 2026-11-21, after the first cloud builder test) — `testing.md`, Git hooks
